*And so I did precisely that. I led a solitary life.*
*I did all my business hidden behind the cottage door.*
*And I sent away anyone who wasn’t here for medicine.*
*Once, a mounted man in armor rode up to the cottage.*
*He looked like a knight, but I had no interest in getting involved with anyone who went around carrying weapons.*
*I was responsible for my own safety, so I had to be careful about who I opened my door to.*
*I had to be extra careful about who I dealt with.*
*But I didn’t feel sad or lonely about it. By making that my “normal,” there was nothing to lament.*
*Which meant old memories served no purpose but to cause me pain.*
*So I endeavored to push them far, far away into the depths of my mind.*
*I couldn’t simply discard my memories of what the lord had done to me, of course, or of all the wonderful people I had met at the brothel.*
*But as time trekked on, they faded into almost nothing.*
*I couldn’t describe what any one person’s voice sounded like, how they acted around me, what kind of person they were,*
*the things we did together, what they looked like, what their name was, anything of importance.*
*I knew good and well it was callous to forget about all the people who had shown me so much kindness and warmth,*
*but I would never see them again, and thinking about them only brought me pain, so there was no point in remembering.*
*It was all I could do to make it by.*
*Nearly four years had passed since I had assumed the role of the lakeside witch.*
*My sixteenth birthday was approaching, but my age was perhaps the only thing I had in common with an ordinary teenage girl.*
*Although if I had grown up like any other girl—having spent my childhood as a saint before being held captive by the lord and made to witness countless people’s deaths—that would probably mean there was something deeply wrong with me.*
*By that point, a terrible cynicism had taken root in my heart. I built high walls around myself, keeping my contact with the outside world as terse and pragmatic as possible.*
*With each passing day, my voice grew more monotonous, my interactions following a well-rehearsed script. My customers didn’t make any attempt to force their way into my shell, either.*
*I’d begun to suspect they might think me not a witch by trade, but that I had actually formed a pact with the Devil and worked his dark magic.*
*No one said as much, but I could sense what seemed to be fear in their voices when they came to me.*
*On the other hand, I never had anyone else try to force themselves into the cottage like the man four years earlier, so I would say I had a rather peaceful life.*
*If you considered a life in which no one wanted anything to do with you “peaceful,” that is.*
*I had also come to learn a fair bit about herbology. I could now make my own medicines, rather than just making do with what was already here when I moved in.*
*Although “a fair bit” still amounted to almost nothing when compared to what my predecessor was capable of. Nonetheless, I had a relatively solid grasp on what herbs to use to treat different symptoms.*
*Enough to formally assume the name of “the lakeside witch,” at least.*
*And so, time flowed on, uneventful and quiet...*
*until one day, when a young man showed up at my door.*
Unknown: "Excuse me... Is anyone home...?"
*I knew right away that he was a new customer. No one who had traded with me before bothered with such pleasantries anymore.*
*They wanted the process to be quick and frictionless, and I preferred it that way as well.*
Morgana: "...Who’s there?"
*When I responded from behind the door, he seemed to be taken aback.*
*After several moments with no answer, I tensed up slightly and repeated the question.*
Unknown: "Ah!"
Unknown: "My sister has fallen very ill, and she desperately needs medicine."
Morgana: "............"
Morgana: "(He has a very pretty voice...)"
Unknown: "I was told that the person who lives here is a skilled herbalist... Could I please get something to treat her?"
Morgana: "...What are her symptoms?"
Unknown: "Ah, uh, she’s got an extremely high fever that won’t come down... She has no appetite—she’s only drinking water— and she can’t get out of bed..."
Morgana: "(Everyone always seems to be looking for medicine for themselves. I haven’t had a customer like him in some time now...)"
Morgana: "We’re going to want to lower her fever first... After that, get her on a nutritious diet. I’m going to give you two medications..."
Morgana: "Give her the first while her fever’s high, and the second after it’s come down..."
Unknown: "Th-Thank you very much..."
Morgana: "............"
Morgana: "(When was the last time someone thanked me?)"
Morgana: "Here’s your medicine. It’s powdered, so mix it with some water and have your sister drink it."
Unknown: "...Um, are you not going to open the door any wider?"
Morgana: "Is that necessary?"
Unknown: "N-No, sorry! Just a second, let me get you your money."
Morgana: "Ah..."
Unknown: "...?"
Morgana: "...I take supplies for my services, not money."
Unknown: "Oh... I didn’t realize... I’m so sorry... This is all I have on me today."
Unknown: "Would you maybe be willing... to accept this as payment today?"
Morgana: "............"
Morgana: "Very well."
Morgana: "(Is... Is that a gold coin?)"
Morgana: "(Those herbs aren’t worth nearly this much...)"
Morgana: "(Just who is this man...?)"
Morgana: "(And what on earth is he doing here, at a witch’s cottage...?)"
*In any other situation, I wouldn’t have even been tempted to look.*
*What if he saw me? He would think me disgusting. Ridicule me like everyone else.*
*It didn’t hurt knowing what people thought of me, but it was hardly pleasant hearing it vocalized either.*
*And what if he decided to do something in that moment I dropped my guard?*
*He could force the door open, have his way with me.*
Morgana: "(...He doesn’t seem like that kind of man, though.)"
*Trust was about the farthest thing from my mind when interacting with people these days. I always remained on high alert, keeping a safe distance from everyone, no matter how “good” they appeared on the surface.*
*But for some reason, I felt strangely comfortable in this young man’s presence.*
*At the time, I couldn’t have explained why,*
*but in retrospect, I imagine it was largely a result of his upbringing.*
*He spoke in a clear, well-enunciated voice. He had a polite, friendly air about him, which I could feel even through the door.*
*It wasn’t the sort of “well-mannered” you could fake with a bit of practice, nor the sort you encountered out in the middle of nowhere.*
*Curiosity took hold of me.*
*I had to know what he looked like.*
*I wasn’t going to throw the door open, of course, and I had no intention of inviting him in either.*
*I just wanted to get a little peek at him through the crack in the door.*
*So I opened it ever so slightly wider than usual when I reached out to take his payment.*
Unknown: "...Ah."
Morgana: "—!"
Morgana: "(Oh no, he saw me—)"
Unknown: "Wow, I didn’t think you would be so young. I’d guessed you were a little older."
Morgana: "...Wha?"
Unknown: "Thank you for doing business with me, and I’m sorry for not coming properly prepared..."
Unknown: "If I need any more, I’ll be sure to bring supplies next time."
Morgana: "A-All right..."
Unknown: "Again, I really appreciate it. Have a nice day."
Morgana: "You too..."
Morgana: "............"
Morgana: "(He... He saw my face... and he wasn’t disgusted... He actually smiled...)"
Morgana: "............"
*I was so caught off-guard I thought for a second that maybe my face had healed.*
*So I ran over to the water basin and, a glimmer of hope in my heart, gazed in at my reflection.*
Morgana: "............"
*But the face looking back at me was the same as always— a grotesque, unsightly, patchy mess.*
*Part of me was disappointed, but more than anything, I was flabbergasted by what had just happened.*
*How could he smile at the sight of this face?*
Morgana: "............"
*For a split second, my mind slipped back in time four years.*
*The man who had applied ointment to my face... he had never seemed disturbed by my appearance either.*
*Not long afterward, the young man called upon my cottage again.*
*He said that the medicine had briefly relieved his sister’s symptoms, but that it had failed to actually cure what ailed her.*
*Herbal medicine required patience and persistence before its effects manifested, I informed him, to which he responded with a sad smile.*
*He was a handsome young man, not far from what I imagined from his voice,*
*and the smile suited him quite well.*
*There was a distinct elegance emanating from him.*
*Ragged clothing aside, he was about how I envisioned a fairy-tale prince might look.*
*Though I knew quite well those only existed in fiction—*
*real nobles were terrible, miserable, reprehensible people.*
*After a few visits, we started having little conversations outside of our business transactions.*
*He told me his name, Mell, as well as his sister’s, Nellie, and I told him mine.*
*Mell was seventeen, only a year older than me.*
*Then one day—*
*he invited me outside for a walk.*
Mell: "It’s not healthy to stay holed up inside all the time."
Mell: "You’ve got this beautiful lake right here. What do you say we take a little walk around the shoreline?"
*I was going to turn him down—*
*but my mouth seemed to disagree with my mind, and the next thing I knew, I was taking a walk with someone for the first time in years.*
*Although “with” might be something of a stretch— I kept a safe distance from him as we walked.*
*Mell seemed disappointed with my unwillingness to get any closer, but I couldn’t bring myself to trust him that much yet.*
*Nonetheless, it was a momentous enough occasion that I felt justified describing it as such.*
*I’m sure I made him uncomfortable on numerous occasions during our walks.*
*To say I acted disagreeably would be an understatement. Any time he closed the gap between us, I stepped a little farther away.*
*Every response I gave was curt, and I repeatedly shot him down whenever he started getting wistful or sentimental.*
*The Morgana of four years prior might have been a little softer with him, a little more human—*
*maybe she would have given him a little smile or a pout instead.*
*But that girl was long gone. I despised the humanity that had once started taking root in me—*
*and I endeavored to be something different again.*
*In one instance, he heard a robin chirping and said, “If I had wings like a bird... maybe I could be free too...” to which I responded:*
Morgana: "There is not a single creature living in this world that is “free.”"
Mell: "Wh—"
Morgana: "Birds regularly get shot down by hunters or preyed on by larger birds."
Mell: "............"
Morgana: "The idea that they are “free” is nothing more than man’s shortsighted delusion."
Mell: "...Ah, uh, sorry... I guess..."
Morgana: "............"
Morgana: "No... I should be apologizing. I wasn’t trying to attack you. I simply lack the ability to properly empathize with others..."
Morgana: "I do not understand people."
Morgana: "So please, if I say anything that upsets you or makes you uncomfortable... don’t assume that was my intention."
Mell: "S-Sure..."
Mell: "Maybe you’re hiding it, or maybe you just don’t know it yet, but I think you’ve got more empathy than you’re letting on."
Mell: "Surely you can’t say nothing affects you at all. You are human."
Morgana: "............"
Morgana: "(At one point, that was true,)"
Morgana: "(though it was perhaps somewhat difficult for others to see...)"
Morgana: "(I felt great beauty in the world when I knelt before the gravestones.)"
Morgana: "(But such sentiments have long since been lost to me.)"
Mell: "So you don’t have to be so... closed off. I might not be able to do much for you,"
Mell: "but I do want to get to know you better. I want to understand you. So if you ever feel like talking about yourself, just know that you’ve got an eager pair of ears here."
Morgana: "............"
Mell: "Okay?"
Morgana: "............"
Morgana: "If there were ever someone who could understand me..."
Morgana: "(If there were ever someone who could understand what I am now...)"
Morgana: "...who could empathize with me..."
Morgana: "(...who had been through trials as great as mine...)"
Morgana: "...they would not be human."
Mell: "Wh-What do you mean...?"
Morgana: "That you would need to be far removed from any idea of humanity to have a chance at understanding me."
Mell: "............"
Morgana: "Regardless,"
Morgana: "I do appreciate your kind words."
Mell: "S-Sure..."
Mell: "............"
Mell: "So, uh... out of curiosity... do you have anyone, um... really important to you in your life?"
Morgana: "............"
Morgana: "Not at the moment..."
Mell: "Does that mean you used to?"
Morgana: "............"
Mell: "I’d love to hear about them... if you don’t mind."
Morgana: "They’re... all long dead."
Mell: "............"
Morgana: "Four years ago... something unpleasant occurred. And as a result of that... I was forced to part ways with the people who taught me that humans have the capability for kindness."
Mell: "Oh, I’m sorry..."
Morgana: "............"
Morgana: "Well then, if your sister’s condition worsens again, come see me. Farewell."
Mell: "H-Hey, uh! Maybe we could walk a little slower next time! So we have more time to talk..."
Mell: "I want to get to know you better..."
Morgana: "............"
Morgana: ".................."
Morgana: "...I’ll consider it."
Mell: "A-All right."
Morgana: "...So long."
Mell: "Ah— Bye... See you soon."
*I was conscious of how cold and distant I was being toward him, but I had no interest in changing anything. Not that I even knew how to anymore.*
*I had never known particularly well how to choose the right words, the proper way to act in various circumstances—*
*but what little knowledge I had possessed I’d long since lost.*
*I assumed, after the things I had said to him, Mell would never ask me on another walk again.*
*But I was wrong—he did the very next time he came for medicine, and every time after that as well.*
Mell: "Hey, uh, so I was thinking... I bet you’d make a really good singer if you wanted."
Morgana: "A... A singer?"
Mell: "Yeah. You’ve got this really pretty voice. I think it’d be nice if you tried using it to sing."
Morgana: "............"
Mell: "...I’m sorry. It’s not my place to tell you what to do. Ahaha..."
Mell: "You hardly talk... why would I think you’d want to sing?"
Morgana: "I’m glad we’re on the same page."
Morgana: "(Never again...)"
Mell: "Sorry about that, really."
Mell: "A-Anyway! I wonder who first came up with the idea to sing. You can brighten someone’s day or sour their mood with just the sound of your voice. Kinda amazing, don’t you think?"
Mell: "It’s almost like magic."
Morgana: "............"
Mell: "Oh, and there are battle songs too, for getting soldiers fired up for combat."
Morgana: "Battle—now there’s a word I never expected to hear from you."
Mell: "I mean, I am a guy. I admire anyone who has the strength to go to battle."
Mell: "I could never, ever do it myself, though. The sight of blood makes me sick, and I barely know which end of a sword you’re supposed to grab."
Morgana: "You’re a noble, aren’t you? What need do you have for knowing how to use a sword?"
Mell: "That’s what I used to think too."
Mell: "But now I think I should have gotten training. I may not have been a soldier, but I needed to be strong."
Mell: "I could have avoided so much if I was..."
Mell: "And Nellie wouldn’t have to live in a rundown shack in the middle of nowhere..."
Morgana: "............"
Morgana: "(As I recall... he said once that he was forced from his house after a dispute over who the rightful heir was.)"
Morgana: "(High society sounds like endless fun.)"
Mell: "Ah, I’m sorry. I’m sure you don’t care about any of this."
Morgana: "No, you’re fine. I’m the one who asked."
Mell: "Thanks..."
Mell: "I want you to know... I really do appreciate it."
Morgana: "...?"
Mell: "It’s nice to just... be able to talk about this stuff to someone."
Morgana: "You’re naturally sociable— I’m sure you could find plenty of other people willing to listen."
Mell: "Mmm..."
Mell: "I don’t get into town much, not even to run errands, so I’m still kind of an outsider..."
Mell: "You’re my only friend right now."
Morgana: "............"
Mell: "Ahhh... I hope that... wasn’t too presumptuous of me."
Morgana: "Call me whatever you like."
Mell: "Oh, thank goodness!"
Mell: "We should do this more often, really. Talk more, get to know each other better, become better friends."
Morgana: "To what end?"
Mell: "No end, just... you know..."
Mell: "Having friends is better than being alone. That’s all."
Morgana: "............"
Morgana: "I see..."
Morgana: "I’ll be heading back in now. See you... again."
Mell: "Yeah. See you soon, Morgana."
Morgana: "............"
*Little by little, his words were getting closer to reaching the old me. Not enough to provoke an actual response, of course—*
*But that wasn’t his fault. I had locked myself up too deeply.*
*I no longer knew how to express emotions— most especially those that resulted from human warmth.*
*However, I had regained enough of something to feel a sort of nostalgia in my interactions with Mell.*
*And I thought perhaps, with enough time...*
*I could go back to who I had been four years prior.*
*But shortly thereafter—*
*he threw all that into the wind with a single utterance:*
Mell: "I’ll take anything at this point to make her better. I’ll even take a miracle if I can get one."
*He appeared exhausted that day, much more than usual. His sister’s condition had begun to worsen again.*
*That one muttered word set off a spark in my mind.*
*It sent me back in time, well beyond when I was taught humanity four years ago, to my very origin.*
*I was the daughter of God.*
*My blood had healing properties.*
*And the instant I remembered that, there was only one course of action I could take.*
*Without a moment’s hesitation, I informed him that miracles did exist. That I had been born a saint,*
*and that my blood had miraculous powers.*
*That after leaving the village of my birth, my power had only ever been misused. First by the lord, for his sick banquets—then by me, for letting it lay idle so long.*
*Now was the time for me to correct that.*
*He was a kindhearted young man who loved his sister. Who deserved this blessing more than them?*
*I told him everything.*
*At first, he was surprised, but never once did he accuse me of lying. He listened carefully and intently, taking in every word.*
*And when I was finished, he said he believed me. He said he would entrust his sister’s care to me.*
*Mell’s sister slept upon a simple bed.*
*She was a sweet little girl who looked like she would have been quite spirited if not for her ailment.*
*Both she and her brother had beautiful flaxen hair. They looked as if they had both been yanked from a painting.*
*Seeing them there, I wanted to do everything I could to help them. To ensure their happiness.*
*And I knew I had the ability to do so.*
*No longer would I be the one receiving others’ charity. I could be the one giving it again.*
*And so I made a new cut on my mostly healed arm.*
*For the briefest of moments, a flash of him applying ointment appeared in my mind, but I quickly cast it off.*
*The flaxen-haired girl showed signs of recovering as a result of my miracle. The boy came to my cottage, overjoyed, to thank me.*
*I was extremely proud of myself. I felt like, for once, I had actually saved someone’s life.*
*I had finally been able to use my gift to help someone truly good.*
*After the miracle, though, he stopped referring to me as his friend.*
*Understandably so.*
*No one could think of themselves as equals with someone who performed miracles. Awe, fear, reverence—they were all feelings that came from the same place:*
*an inability to think of me as a “human” who experienced the world the same way he did.*
*He saw me as... abnormal.*
*I had always been fine with that. It was how it was supposed to be.*
*Being different was what I always wanted, and in some way, it was what had kept me going.*
*So seeing him put more and more distance between us...*
*shouldn’t have bothered me in the slightest.*
*But I continued to offer his sister my blood, no matter how distant he grew.*
*Eventually, he stopped inviting me on walks.*
*But that didn’t matter. I would serve my duty and perform miracles regardless.*
*He would simply knock on my cottage door and say,*
Mell: "Um... my sister’s fallen ill again... Would you come perform another miracle...?"
*I could hear the apprehension in his voice.*
*No longer did he show me his gentle smile. No longer did we listen to the birds singing together. No longer was I the one being cold and distant—*
*and no longer did he say “see you again” when we parted ways.*
*Back in my village, when everyone feared and revered me as a saint...*
*it filled me with a childish sense of superiority.*
*But there was no superiority now. Only a prickling in the softened shell of my heart.*
*I was no longer the child I had once been. I had changed—*
*I had learned what warmth felt like. My memories of that time were vague and ethereal,*
*but the feelings they had created lived on, deep within me.*
*For a time, I had felt a warm familiarity around Mell, which only made me yearn for him to treat me that way again.*
*I wanted to be able to open up to someone once more.*
*Though I never said as much out loud...*
*Gloomy day after gloomy day passed, and then,*
*I received a new visitor.*
*I asked them their business from behind the door, but they hesitated for a few moments before responding.*
*My instincts told me that whoever it was, it wasn’t the noble boy.*
*So I waited, perplexed and slightly on edge, for them to say something.*
*And when they finally did—*
*I was left aghast at what I heard.*
Toyo: "Are you the witch with the miracle blood?"
Morgana: "...!"
*Why did he—*
*Why did he sound like the man... who had slaughtered the slaves in the carriage... laughing all the while?*
*What was he doing at my cottage?*
*I thought he had wandered off somewhere, never to be seen again.*
*I thought he was out of my life for good.*
*Why—*
*Why did he call me a witch?!*
*And how did he know my blood had powers?!*
*Had Mell told him?!*
*Or maybe his talkative little sister had gone running around blabbing about it!*
*Ahh... I should have been clearer with them... made absolutely certain they knew to keep their mouths shut!*
Morgana: "Please leave."
*It took all my strength to get those two words out without my voice shaking.*
*I could hear the screams from four years ago, echoing in my mind.*
*I could see the limbs flying, the blood pooling at my feet.*
*My heart was pounding so hard I could feel it pulsing in my ears.*
Morgana: "Please stay away from my home."
*After all that the time I’d spent forgetting about everything...*
*closing myself off... finally finding peace...*
*why did this monster, who reminded me only of senseless violence and death, have to come back into my world?*
*I never wanted to go through anything like that again.*
Toyo: "I am in need of your blood. Could you not spare a little?"
Morgana: "I have nothing to spare for a man like you."
Morgana: "My blood is only for the sick and needy!"
Morgana: "So please, go away!"
Toyo: "............"
Morgana: "Stay away from me... I’m begging you!"
Toyo: "............"
Morgana: "Get away from this place!"
Toyo: "............"
Morgana: "You’re a murderer! I want nothing to do with you! So please, just go away!"
Toyo: "...As you say."
Morgana: "Nnh..."
Morgana: "............"
Morgana: "(Is... Is he gone...?)"
Morgana: "Ah, ahh..."
Morgana: "(Oh, thank heavens...)"
Morgana: "............"
Morgana: "Ah..."
Morgana: "(My eyes... feel like they’re burning...)"
Morgana: "(No, Morgana... You have to be strong...)"
Morgana: "(You cannot... let this get to you...)"
Morgana: "(B-Be strong...)"
Morgana: "(You have... nothing to fear...)"
Morgana: "(Nothing to fear...)"
Morgana: "Ngh..."
Morgana: "Nnh, nnnnh...!"
Morgana: "(Ah, no, stop... fight it back... ngh...)"
Morgana: "Nnh... nhhh... nrh..."
Morgana: "Ngh... ahhh... aaaaah..."
Morgana: "Aaaaah, nnh, nnnnnh... nrrrrh...!"
Morgana: "Nnnaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaah!"
Morgana: " Aaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaah!"
Morgana: " Waaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaah!"
*I had sworn never to cry again,*
*but the moment fear released its grip on me, everything that had built up came pouring out in a hot stream...*
*Tears soaked my face.*
*I wiped and I wiped and still my hands came back wet.*
*With each passing second, I felt myself reverting into an ordinary, helpless little girl.*
*No—*
*that’s what I had always been.*
*My whole life, I had just been putting on airs...*
*when in reality, I wasn’t even strong enough to withstand a little fright.*
*Even with the horrid man gone, I couldn’t rest easy.*
*I couldn’t be sure if he was gone for good—maybe he was just waiting for a more opportune moment to target me again.*
*The following day, I took a brief walk with the noble boy,*
*but I couldn’t bring myself to talk to him about what had happened.*
*With how much distance he was keeping between us, I doubted he would be willing to do anything to help.*
*For three days and three nights, I shook in terror, thinking, considering, speculating, wondering. Hardly getting a wink of sleep.*
*I was too anxious to sit still, so I paced in circles around the chair by the fireplace.*
Morgana: "It’s too soon to be sure I’m safe..."
Morgana: "Can’t go outside unless I absolutely must..."
Morgana: "Not even just to get water from the lake..."
Morgana: "He could be waiting in the shadows to take my life..."
Morgana: "I’m not safe here anymore..."
Morgana: "I shouldn’t have said anything about my powers..."
Morgana: "They’ve only ever brought me trouble..."
Morgana: "I should probably find somewhere else to live..."
Morgana: "But where...?"
Morgana: "How far could I even get on my own?"
Morgana: "He could be out there, waiting for me to open the door..."
Morgana: "I would need help..."
Morgana: "............"
Morgana: "Don’t be a fool."
Morgana: "No one’s going to help me..."
Morgana: "The boy barely even wants to talk to me anymore..."
Morgana: "All I wanted..."
Morgana: "was a quiet life..."
Morgana: "............"
Morgana: "—?!"
Morgana: "(Is it him?! Did he return?!)"
Morgana: "(C-Calm yourself... He knocks harder than that...)"
Morgana: "(It’s not him...)"
Morgana: "............"
Morgana: "Who’s there?"
Mell: "Um... it’s me..."
Morgana: "............"
Morgana: "(Ahh... his sister needs more of my blood...)"
Mell: "I-I’m not here... about Nellie today. I, um..."
Mell: "I’m sorry for being so distant lately..."
Morgana: "(...What?)"
Mell: "I want to talk things over..."
Morgana: "............"
Morgana: "........................"
Morgana: "(Ahh...)"
Morgana: "(he’s finally reconsidered...)"
Morgana: "(If he can... If he can treat me like a friend again...)"
Morgana: "(then maybe I could ask him...)"
Morgana: "I..."
Morgana: "I want to talk to you too..."
*I opened the door without a second thought.*
*I let my guard drop,*
*because in my mind, the flaxen-haired boy...*
*briefly took the place of the slave man from the brothel.*
*The man who had saved me from the terrible lord, offered me his hand, guided me into the light.*
*And here he was again, to set me free.*
*To take me away from this place, take me somewhere new.*
*He had pulled me out of the darkest depths of despair,*
*and here he was again,*
*ready to offer me his hand,*
*a glimmer of hope to cast away the shadows.*
*So I reached out to take it—*
*and my arm went tumbling to the ground, my hope following not far behind.*
Morgana: "Wh— Ah... aaah..."
Morgana: "My— arm—"
Morgana: "—Ngh, agh... ahh!"
Morgana: "GRAAAAAAAAAAAAH!"
Morgana: "Rrgh, ngh, gh, aaaah... AAAAAAAAAAAAH!  AAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAA AAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAA

*This wasn’t what I had wanted at all.*
*The future I had dreamed of... The outcome I had hoped for.*
*But who did I even have to explain that to?*
*And who would even understand if I tried?*
*There were walls as high as the eye could see on both sides of this path.*
*Forward was the only way I could go.*
*I had killed my old friends and comrades...*
*lost everything along the way... but still I had marched on.*
*Because that was all I could do.*
*And now, power was the only thing that remained in my hands.*
*So I would make a despot of myself,*
*bury all my feelings for her,*
*and continue my march straight into Hell.*
*I had no other choice...*
*Every day felt like a slog through a swamp with no end in sight, though I did everything to keep up appearances when I wasn’t alone.*
*I masked any trace of my discord beneath a veil of bottomless confidence.*
*It had reached a point where nearly everything I said or did in public was an act.*
*A farce, even.*
*Among the “mistakes” the Far-Eastern dog had made was involving a third party.*
*He was a young man, and there was a considerable possibility he was nobility, which meant I couldn’t simply dispose of the boy until I had more information.*
*Between that and his decision to cut off Morgana’s arm, I was looking like quite a fool for thinking I could tame a beast.*
*Though of course, my biggest mistake was coming up with the scheme in the first place.*
*Soon after locking Morgana up in the tower, I summoned the boy,*
*making it abundantly clear he was not to tell anyone what he knew,*
*then sealing the deal by turning him into a co-conspirator.*
*The boy was irritatingly timid, in part, I presumed, because the dog had already threatened him.*
*All it took to keep him nice and obedient was a stern glare and a few choice words.*
*I hired the dog to be my “bodyguard” and assigned the boy to an administrative supervising post on the new church grounds.*
*Having been originally built as a second manor for the previous lord, the church was still more mansion than church.*
*Which meant there was an abundance of rooms for the boy stay in.*
*I then summoned the Saintess there to serve as the face of the church. She was to remain on the grounds all day.*
*And at night, she would stay in a cottage a safe distance from the church.*
*I also informed her that I stored my money and valuables in the observation tower, and that she would be charged with treason if she trespassed.*
*She was a naturally trusting woman, and she seemed grateful for everything I was doing for her, so I doubted she would spill anything.*
*Though even if she did, three keys were required to enter the tower— one held by me, one by the dog, and one by the boy.*
*So all three of us were in this until the witch died.*
*I had one of my aides dig around for information on the noble boy.*
*Mell was his name, and he claimed he had been banished from his home, but I couldn’t just take him at his word.*
*I had to be absolutely sure, so that when the witch died...*
*I could eliminate him.*
*The dog too, of course. No one who knew anything could be allowed to live.*
*That said, he seemed like he could put up a better fight than the boy, so I would have to be a little more careful in choosing how to take him out.*
*I wasn’t too worried, though—*
*I was the lord, after all.*
*So long as that much didn’t change, I could do just about anything.*
*Once we started distributing the miracle elixir, the church’s following grew far quicker than I had expected.*
*Numerous reports of people being cured of diseases spread through the city, and before long, even the healthy came seeking its powers.*
*Every smiling face that walked out of the chapel put another black stain on my spirit.*
*Her blood really was miraculous.*
*And it was then that I understood why the last Barnier had held all those banquets.*
*He wasn’t just torturing a little girl—he was feasting on miracles.*
*And now that word was spreading and I knew the rumors were true,*
*I had lost my final escape route— the plan failing entirely because her blood was just ordinary blood.*
*Forward was the only route that remained.*
*One day, the Far-Eastern dog said to me,*
*“Everything is proceeding exactly as you said it would, lord.”*
*“I didn’t believe in miracles, but seeing all this has tempted me to change my mind.”*
*“You were right—”*
*“one girl’s life is indeed a worthy exchange.”*
*I wasn’t sure how I responded.*
*Though by that point, I largely just rattled off bitter snark and insults reflexively in response to anything.*
*So presumably, I called the dog a dog, perhaps giving a laugh.*
*Most nights, I could no longer sleep without the aid of alcohol.*
*I was pretty sure none of it was poisoned, but I spent a lot of time throwing it back up regardless.*
*Barnier’s cackling echoed in my chambers.*
*The world twisted and contorted.*
*Quite frankly, I was considering taking my own life.*
*But I wouldn’t let myself run away from what I had done. What I was doing to her.*
*I had said I would continue my march straight down to Hell, and that was exactly what I planned to do.*
*Again and again I repeated to myself:*
*“I feel nothing for her.”*
*“I never loved her.”*
*“She’s just another faceless peasant.”*
*“I have no memories...”*
*“...of my past life.”*

*It had been four years since the raid on the brothel, and we were still just barely getting back on our feet.*
*It would never be the same as before, though. Not because we had to move locations,*
*but because the slums were shrinking away to almost nothing thanks to the lord’s “renovations”—*
*not to mention all the new foreign women who sold their services up in the city’s bustling marketplace.*
*Most of our old regulars had taken their business there, and we weren’t drawing in much in the way of new blood either.*
*With better options to get their rocks off, only the real oddballs went for the rundown whorehouse in a little back alley in the slums.*
*It was enough to keep us afloat, but we’d hardly last another six months. A year if we were real lucky.*
*And while we were down here fighting to make it by day-to-day, our ever-gracious lord was planning a festival to celebrate the spring harvest.*
*Celebrations were to take place in and around the new church set up across the river, and with this being the first annual harvest festival, the whole city was in a rather jolly mood.*
*Everyone except us slum rats, of course.*
*The merchants, the upper class, and even most of the regular citizens were in love with the lord.*
*Public opinion took a huge turn after the new church opened its doors.*
*I presumed it was all part of his grand plan.*
*Me, on the other hand—I was far from pleased.*
*He had abandoned us in the slums.*
*Left all his old friends out to dry because he wanted a little extra gold. And like hell I was going to stand for that.*
*Word through the grapevine was the lord and his bodyguard spent a lot of time at the new church.*
*A nun everyone called the “Saintess” worked there, handing out some kind of “miracle elixir.” I assumed it was her that he had business with.*
*But his business didn’t matter so much as the fact that he was there.*
*There was no way I was getting through the fortifications around his manor on the hill,*
*but the church wouldn’t be nearly as well protected.*
*Last night, I snuck onto the grounds to survey the area, and there was no sign of any guards—or people at all.*
*So it wouldn’t likely be difficult for me to slip into the building and hide there until he showed up.*
*Tonight, I brought everything.*
*I was going all in.*
*............*
*These past four years, my feelings for him have been, well, mixed... to put it lightly.*
*I knew damn well he wasn’t the same man I grew up with,*
*but a part of me deep down... wanted to believe that man was still there.*
*That maybe... he had some reason, and I just wasn’t seeing it...*
*That I was misinterpreting him somehow...*
*But if I was right, and the old Jacopo was gone...*
*............*
*I would settle it once and for all tonight.*

*While waiting for the sun to rise, me and the little man hashed out our plan of attack.*
*The lord kept a skilled bodyguard around at almost all times, so we wanted to avoid causing any trouble while they were together.*
*He was only alone for a small window of time.*
*When he arrived at the church each morning, he would first head for his private study, unguarded.*
*That was our chance.*
*Time was limited, which meant we’d make it simple, move quickly.*
*We had the element of surprise on our side, though.*
*I could do this.*
*No, there was no room for even the slightest uncertainty.*
*I would do this.*
*I didn’t care about saving the people from an oppressive despot or anything grand and honorable like that.*
*All I wanted... was to stop an old friend...*
*from falling any further.*
*That was my entire reason for assassinating the lord of the land.*
*I wouldn’t be a hero if I pulled it off—I’d be an insurgent.*
